An error is reported on port e0-0 with value 1; this means that on that interface a
device is connected, but its presence is not provided by the project. As in this case,
erroneous or malicious connections to the network can be easily discovered.

Other notable TTCMP project related commands are:

      A@SW-Std.03-00-DC>sysadmin reset global project

that sets all the TTCMP enabled reachable devices in the network to a "no active
project" status (all the devices discard the configuration obtained from the project)
and makes the TTCMP® subsystem going on with the monitoring activities only.

The same action can be limited to the current switch only:

A@SW-Std.03-00-DC>sysadmin reset local project

162/253                                         Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                                                                                   TTCMP®

     NOTE

     Until all the devices composing the Network are not started and operational,
     the automatic discovery process is somewhat more cautious because it has to
     take into account the presence of some potential closed hardware bypass.

     As soon as the totality of the devices is operational, the discovery process is
     expedited.

19.2.4 Project confirmation

The TTCMP project confirmation action (configuration "freezing") can be triggered
from one of the TTCMP enabled switches of the network by means of the command:

      A@sw10>sysadmin set topology status confirmed

The permanent change to the confirmed status is acknowledged by the LED n. 3 on the
device that turns steady green.

19.2.5 Operations feedback

Some information about the status of the TTCMP® subsystem are visually presented to
the user by means of some of the Consist Switch's LEDs:

    · LED n. 2
          off
              TTCMP disabled
          slow blinking green
              TTCMP running but no match found (either no active project or switch
              trying to match)
          fast blinking green
              The switch is matched and is waiting for the other switches to match;
              configuration will be loaded in seconds
          steady green
              The switch is matched and the configuration applied
          steady red
              Fault or anomaly detected

    · LED n. 3
          steady green
              The switch status is confirmed

    · LED n. 4

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  163/253
TTCMP®

          steady red
              The status of the switch is confirmed but it cannot be configured since the
              project does not define any device matching the current hostname

19.3 Commands summary

The following is the reference list of TTCMP® available commands from the CLI.
Parameter names are in all-caps italic. Optional arguments are enclosed in square
brackets.
All commands, except for the show group, support the no-form.

      configure lldp ttcmp extensions enable

Turn on/off the TTCMP® subsystem.

      configure ttcmp auto confirm enable

Turn on/off the automatic TTCMP® confirmation of new devices inserted into an
already confirmed network.

      configure ttcmp auto cst-id enable

Turn on/off the automatic selection of the announced Consist UUID among the ones
announced by the devices belonging to the same consist.

      show ttcmp

Display information about TTCMP protocol.

     show topology [ detail [ device <NAME> ]]

List a table where all the TTCMP® capable devices in the network segment are shown
together with their TTCMP® information.
The optional detail parameter produces a verbose output.
The produced information is limited to a specific switch if a device clause is specified.

      show counters protocol ttcmp

Display various counters related to each different TTCMP® message type.

     configure snmp-server traps-ttcmp <TRAP-TYPE>

Set one or more SNMP trap to be emitted by the device.
Currently the only TTCMP® trap defined is ttcmp-anomaly.

      clear counters protocol ttcmp

Clear all the TTCMP® counters.

      clear topology

Clear all the topology information gathered by the device.

164/253  Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                                                                                   TTCMP®

      sysadmin show projects

List all the TTCMP projects loaded in the device.

     sysadmin set global project <PROJECT-ID>

The TTCMP project identified by PROJECT-ID is selected, spread to all other TTCMP
capable devices and set as the current active project.

      sysadmin set topology match-mode default|default-fast|diffusion|none

Select and activate a TTCMP® matching algorithm.

      sysadmin set topology status confirmed

Device configuration is permanently saved on flash so that no subsequent matching
activity is needed.

     sysadmin reset global project

Disable the current active project on every switch in the network.

      sysadmin reset local project

Disable the current active project on the local switch only.

     sysadmin set cst-id [uuid/]<VALUE>

Set the consist identifier (Consist ID).
If the optional uuid/ prefix is specified then VALUE is parsed as a UUID. Otherwise
VALUE is supposed to be plain text and the ASCII values of its composing characters are
stored.

      sysadmin reset cst-id

Reset the consist identifier (Consist ID).
The factory UUID becomes the value announced by the device.

     sysadmin delete project <PROJECT-ID>

The specified TTCMP project is deleted from the device.

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  165/253
                                                                               Coupled-Switch© mode

Chapter 20 - Coupled-Switch© mode

20.1 Introduction

The Coupled-Switch© mode is an advanced configuration option that allows the
aggregation of two switches into a single switching system named Coupled-Switch©
that can then be connected to other Coupled-Switches© resulting in a ladder physical
topology.

A Coupled-Switch© is perfectly suitable to provide a solution to the constraints
commonly found on trains allowing the design of loop-free topologies overcoming the
limitation of classical spanning tree designs and providing a deterministic sub-second
recovery time in case of single or multiple link or switch failure.

Coupled-Switch© mode offers the following key advantages:

    · no need of spanning-tree protocol

    · deterministic sub-second recovery time independent from network size

    · no need for any special adapter or special cable for connecting the pair

    · handle multiple concurrent switch/cable failures

    · doubled bandwidth available over the ladder backbone

    · integrated with the TTCMP management protocol allows the replacement of a
         faulty member with the automatic configuration of the new device

Since the switch pair share their data plane but not the control plane both members
remain autonomous, use their own CPU, have their own management IP address and
are viewed as separate devices on the TTCMP topology tables.

20.2 How to connect the switches

The ports used to form a Coupled-Switch© pair are fixed.

Ports e0-2 and e0-3 are used to link together the switch pair: port e0-2 on master
device should be connected with port e0-2 on slave device, and port e0-3 on master
with port e0-3 on slave. These Ethernet ports are aggregated together and they are
used as a backplane to connect the two switching engines.

Ports e0-0 and ports e0-1, on both members, are used to connect to another Coupled-
Switch©. Each of these ports, commonly referred as "backbone" ports, is aggregated
with the correspondent port on the partner device forming two distinct distributed
groups, one for each direction of the ladder.

The resulting structure is depicted in the following drawing.

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  167/253
Coupled-Switch© mode

In order to guarantee the maximum level of resiliency, also in case of a power failure,
the backbone lines can be protected using a hardware bypass between the two
backbone ports.

20.3 Coupled-Switch© Coupling mode
    configuration

The only command required to enable the Coupled-Switch© mode is the following:

      configure coupled-mode role master|slave [left-only|right-only]

When this command is run, some changes on the active configuration are
automatically executed:

    · structural ports e0-0, e0-1, e0-2 and e0-3 are removed from spanning tree
         control

    · ports e0-2 and e0-3 are linked together and the ist0 (Inter-Switch Trunk)
         interface is created

    · backbone ports e0-0 and e0-1 are disabled, waiting for members
         synchronization

    · a synchronization protocol is started on the Inter-Switch Trunk.

    · as soon as the coupled partners are synchronized, or after a predefined
         timeout period, the backbone ports are re-enabled.

All the information related to the Coupled-Switch© mode status can be obtained
issuing the show coupled mode command, like in the following example:

168/253               Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                                 Coupled-Switch© mode

A@SW-Std.03-00-DC>show coupled-mode

Coupled mode is enabled
Node type: middle/master
Backbone aggregation protocol: static

Inter-switch connections
=====================================

   Partner address: a0:59:3a:03:00:fa
   Connection 0 (e0-2) is up
   Connection 1 (e0-3) is up
   Synchronization status: SYNC_OK

Distributed ports
==========================================

       (L -Local port P - Partner port)

          name type     line           status

       ----------------------------------------

       L e0-0 backbone  up             active

       P  backbone      up             active

       L e0-1 backbone  up             active

       P  backbone      up             active

The most important field is the synchronization status, SYNC_OK means that the
Coupled-Switch© is fully operational and that it can start operating as a single
switching engine.

The possible values for this field are reported on the following table:

Value                Meaning

SYNC_PRE0            Initial state, ready to start the synchronization protocol.
SYNC_SO              First step of the initial handshake, waiting for partner acknowledge.
SYNC_CHECK           Some trouble on IST links or on partner member.
SYNC_CHECK_SENT      This member is attempting to reconnect with its partner.
SYNC_OK              Fully operational.

If we consider a Coupled-Switch© pair as a reference point, then it may be connected
with other Coupled-Switch© pairs on the right and/or on the left side.

A right connected Coupled-Switch© have its backbone ports (either e0-1 or e0-0)
connected with the e0-0 ports of the reference Coupled-Switch© pair.

A left connected, on the other hand, have its backbone ports (either e0-1 or e0-0)
connected to the e0-1 ports of the reference.

If no Coupled-Switch© pairs will be connected on the left of the reference, it shall be
configured as "coupled right" (adding the right-only option in the command). Vice-
versa if no other pairs will be connected on its right side, the reference shall be
configured as "coupled left" (adding the left-only option in the command).

20.4 Backbone aggregation protocol

The backbone ports e0-0 and e0-1 of each coupled-switch members are aggregated
with their respective ports of the partner device in a distributed group. Both members

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4                                    169/253
Coupled-Switch© mode

are active and the backbone traffic is distributed across them using a hashing function
of the source and destination MAC addresses.

The status of these ports is also reported by the show coupled command.

These distributed groups can be statically or dynamically aggregated. By default the
aggregation protocol for the backbone lines is "static", meaning that the bonding/un-
bonding of group members relies only on their physical link status. This could lead to
some reachability issues when a Coupled-Switch© member, protected with a
hardware bypass, is switched off since on its neighbors the backbone link remain up.

In these cases the CMAP protocol (Coupled Mode Aggregation Protocol) can be
enabled on backbone lines. The main role of this protocol is to detect and resolve the
issue described on the previous section. Each Coupled-Switch© have a unique
identifier, automatically defined by combining the MAC addresses of both members.
This identifier is transmitted inside the protocol payload allowing neighbors to detect
the status of asymmetrical connection, due to the bypass action, and set to stand-by
the backbone port connected through the bypass.

Beside of this the CMAP protocol is also capable to detect connectivity issues on the
rx/tx path that does not cause a physical link failure.

The CMAP protocol is enabled by issuing, on both members, the command:

      A@SW-Std.03-00-DC>configure coupled-mode cmap enable

When enabled, protocol status information is reported on the output of the show
coupled command:

      [...]
      CMAP status

          ==========================================
              backbone port e0-0: port is down
              backbone port e0-1: running

Note that if the right-only or left-only option is not set, the protocol is started on
both sides of the Coupled-Switch©.

20.5 Coupled-Switch© Dual home

A high performance end-device, e.g. a media streaming server, may need to be
connected to the network with redundant links for high performance and high-
availability reasons.

In the Coupled-Switch© mode two ports can be aggregated not only locally, taking
advantage of the Link Aggregation features (cfr. Link Aggregation), but also in a
distributed way, so that the end-device could benefit of both the redundancy and the
aggregation of the two links.

The server is said to be "dual homed".

Statistically, the traffic along the ladder in a Coupled-Switch© topology is sent on both
rails so the total throughput of such a topology is more or less 2 times the nominal
speed of the ports.

170/253  Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                       Coupled-Switch© mode

Dual homed end-devices may benefit from this fact being able to actually deliver data
at the full speed through the two links, maintaining the ability to send all the traffic on
one of the two links in case of a fault of the other line.

The member ports of a dual home connection must be the same on the two switches
of a Coupled-Switch© pair. Not all the ports may be part of a dual home pair but only
e1-0, e1-1, e1-2, e1-3, e1-4, e2-0 and e2-1 (if available).

The type of aggregation realized by default on the dual home pair is "static, balanced",
so it can be used with any kind of balanced packet sending set in the end-device, as for
example the "balance-xor" mode in the Linux bonding driver.

The command to set a port in dual home mode is:

      A@SW-Std.03-00-CA>configure interface e2-0 switchport dual-home

This command must be input on both the switches of a Coupled-Switch© pair.

During the setup phase a LED will blink to signal the synchronization phase. As long as
the two ports are joint, non more LED flashing will happen.

     NOTE

     Due to the nature of the aggregation mode used, the e0-0 and the e0-1 ports,
     if enabled, may be used as a dual home pairs. So, for example, in the above
     picture, instead of a Coupled-Switch© pair on the e0-0 ports couple of the
     rightmost pair, a dual homed media streaming server could be connected.
     The same could be done with the e0-1 ports couple of the leftmost Coupled-
     Switch© pair.

20.5.1 Support for LACP protocol on dual home ports

Beside being configured for static aggregation, dual home ports can also be
configured for dynamic negotiation with the partner device by means of the standard
LACP protocol.
However, since Coupled-Switch© architecture does not support the spanning-tree
protocol, unbounded members are automatically moved to STP blocking state in order
to avoid Ethernet loops.

We refer to LACP on dual-home ports as DLACP (Distributed LACP), due to the
distributed nature of this kind of setup.

LACP can be enabled while configuring the dual home ports using the following
command on both the coupled members:

      configure interface <if-name> switch port dual-home lacp [fast | slow]

The DLACP subsystem behavior when the coupled members are out-of-sync can be set
with the command:

      configure coupled-mode dlacp mode-on | mode-sync

It specifies whether or not LACP packets should be transmitted/received if the

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  171/253
Coupled-Switch© mode

Coupled-Switch© is out-of-sync. The default mode is mode-sync (packets not
transmitted/received of out-of-sync).

This setup aim to force member unbonding on partner device in case of missing
coupled synchronization.

The show coupled-mode command reports all the information related to the status of
the DLACP subsystem.

As depicted in the following example, if the DLACP protocol succeeds in negotiating
the aggregation with the partner, the status of the link is reported as bndl, otherwise it
is reported as standby.

show coupled-mode
   Coupled mode is enabled
   Node type: middle/master
   Backbone aggregation protocol: CMAP
   DLACP global mode: on

Inter-switch connections
=====================================

   Partner address: a0:59:3a:80:c2:10
   Connection 0 (e0-2) is up
   Connection 1 (e0-3) is up
   Synchronization status: SYNC_OK

Distributed ports
==========================================

(L -Local port P - Partner port)

         name type    line              status

----------------------------------------

L e0-0 backbone       up                active

P        backbone     up                active

L e0-1 backbone       up                active

P        backbone     up                active

L e1-3 dual-home down                   -

P        dual-home down                 -

L e1-4 dual-home down                   -

P        dual-home down                 -

L e2-0 dual-home up                     standby

P        dual-home up                   bndl

CMAP status
==========================================

   backbone port e0-0: running
   backbone port e0-1: running

172/253                                          Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                                                                     Coupling solutions

Chapter 21 - Coupling solutions

This chapter describes the solutions available on Consist Switch for supporting the
joining of two or more trains together.
These solutions are referred as "TTCMP for Backbone" or T4B.

21.1 TTCMP for Backbone (T4B)

TTCMP for Backbone (T4B) takes advantage of the capabilities offered by the TTCMP
protocol to provide a solution to one of the most common requirements of the railway
operators: handling the coupling/decoupling of two or more trains.

21.1.1 TCDS

The T4B main component is the TTCMP Train Composition Discovery Service (TCDS); this
is the service in charge of the automatic discovery of the active consists composition.
Consists are sequences of indivisible items (cars, coaches, trains).
Since each consist has its own Ethernet Consist Network (ECN), the result of a new
composition will always be a new bigger or smaller ethernet network.
T4B provides two different modes for interconnecting ECN's:

    · plain;
    · routed.
In plain mode all the ECN's are directly connected and share a common broadcast
domain and IP network for each one of the defined VLANs.
In routed mode all the ECN's are logically separated into different IP networks so inter-
consist traffic must be somehow routed at IP level.
The service works properly if the following conditions are enforced on switches
configuration and train topology:
    · the train composition is made of a linear sequence of consists;
    · all the switches in the same consist shares a common consist identifier (cst-id);
    · ethernet ports connecting different consists are configured as "boundary

         ports";
    · each boundary port must be statically associated with a specific direction DIR1

         or DIR2 that will be used to understand each consist orientation respect to the
         current composition reference direction;
    · the boundary port attribute can be configured on standard access ports
         associated to any VLAN as well as on ports configured as trunks;

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  173/253
Coupling solutions

    · a switch with one or more ports configured as boundary port is named
         "boundary switch".

The following picture summarizes the key elements and their roles:

                   Boundary switch  switch  switch Boundary switch
Boundary port                                                                     Boundary port

Boundary port                       switch  switch                          Boundary port
                  Boundary switch                   Boundary switch

The TCDS service can be started on any node using the command:

      configure tcds enable

TCDS service startup is mandatory in all the boundary switches. Optionally it can also
be enabled in all the other switches in order to have the composition information
available in every node. In non boundary switches TCDS does not play any active role
but just listens and collects the messages exchanged by the boundary switches.

21.1.2 Consist boundaries

As stated before, the ports that can be used for coupling purposes constitute the
boundary of a consist and must be configured accordingly using the following
interface command:

      configure interface NAME tcds boundary-port direction dir1 | dir2

If IEC61375-2-5 compatibility is required, the boundary ports can also be labeled with
one of the two supported lines (A or B) using the following interface command:

      configure interface NAME tcds line-id A | B

Depending on the coupling logic used (plain or routed), boundary ports could also be
configured as access ports for a specific VLAN or as trunk ports capable of carry traffic
for multiple VLANs.

Note that boundary ports only inhibit TTCMP packets from entering the consist while
they do not interfere in any way with all the other network traffic entering or exiting
the port.

This behavior can be altered by the command

      configure tcds ttcmp forwarding

that allows all the TTCMP traffic to enter a boundary port and flow through the consist
while preserving the correct handling of the packets. This is useful, for example, when
the complete TTCMP network topology is required to be known by any device,

174/253                                     Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                                                   Coupling solutions

regardless of the consist (i.e. in a train configured using a multi-coach project).

All the consist switches must be configured with the same consist-id since they are all
members of the same "entity". Both the available formats (string and uuid) for
configuring the consist-id can be used for this purpose.

21.2 Local consist discovery

The first activity performed by the TCDS service at startup is the local consist
properties discovery (boundary switches, boundary ports and associated directions).

This activity is based on the periodical transmission of Ethernet messages from each of
the configured boundary switches. These messages are called "Internal CST
announcements". They are transmitted within the consist and do not cross its
boundaries.

Each node with an active TCDS service collects the data carried by these
announcements and builds a dynamic representation of the local consist.

The information describing the local consist can be inspected using the command:

show tcds local

that reports the local consist status as follows:

Using automatic consist-id election
     Consist-id election process is completed
     Selected consist-id is: 00d10061-2498-4762-9783-5a2f41855d55

Boundary switches discovered inside this consist: 4
Active TCDS mode is: plain
Forwarding of TTCMP traffic on boundaries is: enabled
Discovery position: current 3 - locked 0 (unlocked)
Device name: B-SW-8 - Type: boundary switch
Sending internal CST announcements at slow frequency

Gathered neighbour information for direction DIR2:
     CstUUID: 32fe4b1b-2599-4283-addd-1e07de7232ae
     Bsw data: A(e0-1) ---> C-SW-10 A(e0-0)

Local consist boundary status:

Direction DIR1

==============================================================================

sw-name          port ID status neighbour cst-id

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

B-SW-6           e0-0 A up-b-fwd 5bb9e0f2-7e08-49e9-9abb-eaa3ab5c4495

B-SW-7           e0-0 B up-b-fwd 5bb9e0f2-7e08-49e9-9abb-eaa3ab5c4495

Direction DIR2

==============================================================================

sw-name          port ID status neighbour cst-id

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

B-SW-8           e0-1 A up-b-fwd 32fe4b1b-2599-4283-addd-1e07de7232ae

B-SW-9           e0-1 B up-b-fwd 32fe4b1b-2599-4283-addd-1e07de7232ae

Train composition table:
     CRC 0x048b4f79 - stable since 3 hours 43 minutes 5 seconds

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4                              175/253
Coupling solutions

        Advertised CST-IDs:
              DIR1 5bb9e0f2-7e08-49e9-9abb-eaa3ab5c4495
              OWN 00d10061-2498-4762-9783-5a2f41855d55
              DIR2 32fe4b1b-2599-4283-addd-1e07de7232ae

        Dropped External CST announcements: 0
        Dropped Internal CST announcements: 0

21.2.1 Automatic consist-id election

In all the situations where the user does not want to deal with consist-id assignment,
an automatic consist-id election is applied. If there is no consist-id configured, the
boundary switches automatically use the UUID value burned into their EEPROM for
their internal CST announcements. The lowest UUID value learned from the internal
CST announcements will then be used as the common consist-id.

     NOTE

     In order to operate properly, this election mechanism needs that none of the
     boundary switches is configured with a user defined consist-id.

The internal CST announcements are initially transmitted at a "fast" frequency, to
speed-up the discovery process, that is turned to "slow" when the discovery/election
process is completed.
To prevent a complete reelection process at each boot, the information about the
elected consist-id is permanently saved on each device. In case of a boundary switch
replacement, the election process is automatically restarted if an internal CST
announcement with a lower consist-id is received.

21.3 Coupling local detection

Coupling begins when one of the boundary ports transitions from down to up.
Neighbor detection is based on standard LLDP packets exchanged between boundary
ports.
These packets report to the connected neighbor some local consist information; if this
information is missing the coupling is not considered valid and an error condition is
associated to the boundary port. In the following example the code 'E' on the "status"
column means "Error" (the others are the link status, up or down, and the spanning
tree port status, blk or fwd).

176/253  Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                                 Coupling solutions

...
...

Direction DIR2

==============================================================================

sw-name         port ID status neighbour cst-id

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

SW-C            e2-0  up-E-fwd
SW-D            e2-0  up-E-blk

Otherwise, when the received information is correct, the show tcds local command
reports the consist-id of the connected consist as depicted in the following example:

...

...

Direction DIR2

===============================================================================

sw-name         port ID status neighbour cst-id

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

SW-C            e2-0  up-b-fwd 2bd16b36-bbed-4521-857f-b883078db682

SW-D            e2-0  up-b-blk 2bd16b36-bbed-4521-857f-b883078db682

In this case the 'b' code means "Boundary ok".

21.4 Composition discovery

While coupled, each boundary switch periodically transmits from its connected
boundary ports an "External CST announcement". These announcements are forwarded
to all the switches of the network and allow the receivers to build a complete
representation of the active train composition.

The core of the discovery logic is the Train Composition Table. This table is filled with
the information contained in the "External CST announcements" (received from all the
consists) and represents the current composition as an ordered and oriented consists
list, starting from the extremity with the lowest consist-id and moving towards the
opposite side.

Consists are numbered starting from the end side with the lowest consist-id, to which
is assigned the number 1. A reference direction is defined so that DIR1 points in the
direction of the consist number 1.

The current active composition can be retrieved using the command:

      show tcds composition

which reports not only the list of discovered consists but also the overall status of the
discovery process as depicted in the following picture:

          Discovery information
          -----------------------

              TCDS discovery is complete
              Active TCDS mode is plain
              TTCMP forwarding is enabled
              Number of discovered consists: 5
              CRC computed on local table: 0x048b4f79

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4            177/253
Coupling solutions

Active Train Composition

--------------------------

            CstNum Cst-id           Orient. CRC

                            Coach ID (Description) H Discovery Lock

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

            1 12345678-9abc-def0-1234-56789abcdef0 Inverse 0x048b4f79

                            2 (---  ) N 0 N 0x00000000

            2 32fe4b1b-2599-4283-addd-1e07de7232ae Inverse 0x048b4f79

                            5 (---  ) N 0 N 0x00000000

         *  3 00d10061-2498-4762-9783-5a2f41855d55 Inverse 0x048b4f79

                            4 (---  ) N 0 N 0x00000000

            4 5bb9e0f2-7e08-49e9-9abb-eaa3ab5c4495 Inverse 0x048b4f79

                            3 (---  ) N 0 N 0x00000000

            5 4127a97a-f4d7-4ad4-8f2b-ce379e430d89 Inverse 0x048b4f79

                            1 (---  ) N 0 N 0x00000000

In every active consist a CRC for the active train composition table is computed and
transmitted to all the other consists by means of the External CST announcements.
When all the consists share the same CRC value, the discovery process can be
considered complete.

The consist we are connected to is reported with a * character at the beginning of the
line.

21.5 TCDS routed mode

While TCDS plain mode is intended to support only the discovery of the current
coupled consists without playing any active role in controlling inter-consist network
traffic (the result is always a single Layer-2 Ethernet domain), TCDS routed mode
introduces a Layer-3 separation among the interconnected consists. This means that
the IP traffic between hosts on different consists must be routed in order to reach its
destination. This behavior, together with an automatic IP address translation, allows to
connect together several trains that share the same IP address space and makes local
consist end-devices accessible from the other consists using "train wide" translated
addresses.

This mode mimics the standard IEC-61375-2-5 logic without requiring a dedicated
cabling for the train-wide Ethernet backbone.

When routed mode is enabled, the TCDS sub-system handles traffic that flows among
the different consists using a user-defined VLAN (the backbone-vlan). At least one of
the switches must be configured with the role of "virtual TBN" (vTBN) in order to
provide the automatic routing and R-NAT (Railway NAT) services.

The steps required to properly configure and enable the TCDS service in routed mode
are summarized below:

    · configure TCDS for routed mode on all the switches

      configure tcds mode routed

    · create and configure the "backbone"VLAN on all the switches

      configure vlan 100 description "backbone vlan"
      configure tcds backbone-vlan 100

178/253                             Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                                                                     Coupling solutions

    · on boundary switches, configure all the boundary ports as access ports for the
         selected backbone VLAN

      configure interface e1-1 switch port mode access vlan 100
      configure interface e1-1 tcds boundary-port direction DIR1

    · on the nodes that should act as virtual TBN, create the SVI interface associated
         to the backbone VLAN; this interface will be automatically configured for IP
         routing and NAT after each coupling/uncoupling event

      configure interface vlan100
      configure interface vlan100 enable
      configure tcds vtbn enable

    · configure an IP address on the SVI interface facing the local consist network;
         the default value (10.0.0.1/18) comes from the standard IEC addressing
         schema, however it can be adapted to an own cutom schema by redefining the
         base templates (as explained next)

    · enable IP routing

      configure ip routing enable

    · finally enable the TCDS protocol on all the nodes

      configure tcds enable

When this setup is in place, the vTBNs automatically reconfigure the IP address of the
backbone virtual interface, the R-NAT rules and the inter-consist routes whenever a
change in the active composition is detected.

The IP addresses used by TCDS for configuring the backbone network are built starting
from some templates (by default following the rules defined in IEC61375-2-5). These
templates can be customized using the commands listed below:

      configure tcds vtbn consist-network <a.b.c.d/n>

This command defines the IP network that is used inside the consist and it is used in
building the R-NAT rules; by default it is equal to 10.0.0.0/18.

      configure tcds vtbn ip-address-template <template>

This template is used in building the IP address assigned to the backbone SVI interface.
Its default value is 10.128.0.[etbn-id]/18 where etbn-id is a placeholder for the
vTBN position in the current composition.

      configure tcds vtbn train-network-template <template>

This last template is used in building the backbone address assigned to each consist
network. The default is 10.128.[subnet-id<<6].0/18 where subnet-id is the
placeholder for the position of the consist network in the active composition.

For example, assuming that two consists configured with the default templates are
connected, a device with local address 10.0.0.20 will be able to reach the
corresponding device located in the other consist using the backbone address
10.128.64.20 or 10.128.128.20 depending on the composition order and direction
(which is driven by the consist-UUID value as explained before).

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  179/253
Coupling solutions

When adopting not standard addressing schemes, the customized templates must be
carefully selected paying attention to avoid overlaps in used network address spaces.
Moreover, since the automatic R-NAT applies a 1-to-1 address translation, the network
mask applied to the local consist network must be the same used for the backbone
addresses.
For example, when configuring the network templates as follows:

      configure tcds consist-network 172.16.1.0/24
      configure tcds vtbn train-network-template 192.168.[subnet-id].0/24

a device with local consist address 172.16.1.10 will be able to reach the
corresponding device located in the other consist using the backbone address
192.168.1.10 or 192.168.2.10.
Additional custom multicast routes can be automatically installed in the vTBN
configuration whenever a change in the active composition is detected. This is
achieved adding, for each custom multicast route, one command like

      configure tcds vtbn mroute <IN-PORT> <ADDRESS> <NETMASK>

where IN-PORT is the ingress port and ADDRESS is the multicast address to match
applying the specified NETMASK.

21.6 Composition Lock

The Composition Lock operation triggers the computation of the position of each
consist in the active composition using the data of the Train Composition Table and
permanently stores it on the device memory and file-system until a Composition
Unlock is performed.
The stored information is an integer number representing the position (starting from
1) of the consist respect to one end of the train. Also the CRC of the Composition Table
at the time of the Lock operation is saved for later reference.
This information survives reboots and it is used at startup, in the configuration phase,
for dynamically building the configured IP addresses that are marked as position
dependent.
An IP address is marked as position dependent if preceded by the @ character. Any
address in the configuration can be marked in this way. For example:

      configure interface vlan1 ip address @172.16.0.1 255.255.255.0

21.6.1 Position dependent addresses mask

The stored position number, if available, is applied at startup to every position
dependent marked address found in the configuration (it does not matter if static or by
TTCMP project). How the position of the consist must be applied for building the IP
addresses is specified by the address composition mask using the following command

180/253  Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                                                                     Coupling solutions

      configure tcds address composition mask <a.b.c.d>

where the a.b.c.d parameter is a sort of netmask with the bits set only in the portion
of the address where the position number is applied.
Supposed the address space of the consist to be 172.16.0.0/16, the requirement is to
apply the position number to the 3rd octet (i.e. an address built in a device belonging to
the 1st consist of the composition will be 172.16.1.X, in a device belonging to the 2nd
consist will be 172.16.2.X and so on). In order to obtain such behavior the address
composition mask to be configured is 0.0.255.0 with the command

      configure tcds address composition mask 0.0.255.0

     NOTE

     If no position number is available (the composition is Unlocked) or when the
     address composition mask is missing, every position dependent marked address
     is applied as it is.

21.6.2 Composition Head flag

The reference direction of the active composition can be reversed respect to the actual
train orientation (since the first consist of the Train Composition Table is the far end
one with the lowest UUID). As a consequence the order of the position number
assignment (ascending or descending) respect to the actual train orientation is
unpredictable. To overcome this issue, a fixed starting point for the enumeration can
be set by means of the Head flag.
The Head flag is an information shared by a consist and used in the Composition Lock
stage to point the consist enumeration in ascending or descending order respect to
the current Train Composition Table. The value of this flag is included in both the
"Internal" and "External CST announcements".
If the first or the last consist of the composition (not more than one) advertises the
Head flag, at the Composition Lock the position number 1 is assigned to that consist and
the enumeration will be in ascending or descending order accordingly.
The command that sets the flag in a device is

      sysadmin set composition head

while the one that removes the flag is

      sysadmin reset composition head

The status of the flag is visible in the output of the show ttcmp command:

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  181/253
Coupling solutions

      A@C-SW-1>show ttcmp

      Global TTCMP information:
          TTCMP protocol version 2 extensions are enabled
          TTCMP subsystem status is: idle
          TTCMP packets signature is: disabled
          TTCMP matching mode is: default
          Consist identifier: undefined
          Coach identifier: 5 (no description)
          Composition Head flag: not set

      ...

      A@H-SW-2>show ttcmp

      Global TTCMP information:
          TTCMP protocol version 2 extensions are enabled
          TTCMP subsystem status is: idle
          TTCMP packets signature is: disabled
          TTCMP matching mode is: default
          Consist identifier: undefined
          Coach identifier: 1 (no description)
          Composition Head flag: set

      ...

Even though it is a good practice to set the value on all the Consist Switchs installed in
the consist that is required to be the "head consist" (position number 1), it is sufficient to
set it on one of its boundary switches.

21.6.3 Lock/Unlock operations

The Composition Lock is the operation responsible of permanently storing the position
number referred to the current composition on each Consist Switch belonging to the
network and having the TCDS enabled. The position will be used later in building the IP
addresses found in the device configuration and marked with the char @ replacing a
part of the address with the position (as defined by the address composition mask).

The position number is stored and used every time the device boots up, until a the
Composition Unlock operation is performed. The Unlock deletes the position
information that, from the next boot, is not available any more.

The Composition Lock/Unlock status and related operations are the following:

    · reset: is the default status of a brand new device and can be set by the
         command

                sysadmin reset composition lock

         It is the only value that is not set train-wide when the command is executed; it
         automatically turns to unlocked or locked when the device is installed as
         replacement to a broken one in an already configured train.

    · unlocked: a position number is not stored nor used in building the IP
         addresses marked with the @ char; if the management address of the device is
         marked, it is dynamically changed according to the current position in the
         composition. This status can be set train-wide issuing the following command
         on a boundary switch of any consist

                sysadmin set composition unlock

182/253             Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                                                                     Coupling solutions

    · locked: a position number is stored and used in building the IP addresses
         marked with the @ char, including the management address of the device (that
         does not change dynamically if the actual composition changes); the position
         is computed according to the presence of the Head flag or, alternatively,
         according to the actual order of the table. This status can be set train-wide
         issuing the following command on a boundary switch of any consist

                sysadmin set composition lock [ direct | reversed | forced ]

          When the direct optional token is specified, the presence of any Head flag
              is ignored and the position is computed according to the order of the
              table.

          When the reversed optional token is specified, the presence of any Head
              flag is ignored and the position is computed reversely respect to the order
              of the table.

          The forced optional token can be used only on devices belonging to the
              first or the last consist of the composition; when specified, the presence of
              any Head flag is ignored, the position of the current consist is set to 1 and
              the position of the other consists is computed accordingly.

The local status of the Composition Lock can be queried by the command

      show tcds local

that reports the local Unlocked status as follows:

        Using automatic consist-id election
              Consist-id election process is completed
              Selected consist-id is: 00d10061-2498-4762-9783-5a2f41855d55

        Boundary switches discovered inside this consist: 4
        Active TCDS mode is: plain
        Forwarding of TTCMP traffic on boundaries is: enabled
        Coach identifier: 3 (First Class)
        Composition Head flag: not set
        Discovery position: current 3 - locked 0 (unlocked)
        Device name: B-SW-8 - Type: boundary switch
        Sending internal CST announcements at slow frequency
      ...

It reports a local Locked direct status as follows:

      ...
        Discovery position: current 2 - locked 2 (direct)

      ...

and a local Locked reversed status as follows:

      ...
        Discovery position: current 2 - locked 4 (reversed)

      ...

The train wide status of the Composition Lock can be queried by the command

      show tcds composition

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  183/253
Coupling solutions

that reports the status of all the consists, as shown in the following example:

Discovery information
-----------------------

   TCDS discovery is complete
   Active TCDS mode is plain
   TTCMP forwarding is enabled
   Number of discovered consists: 5
   CRC computed on local table: 0x048b4f79

Active Train Composition

--------------------------

            CstNum Cst-id                   Orient. CRC

                            Coach ID (Description) H Discovery Lock

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

            1 12345678-9abc-def0-1234-56789abcdef0 Inverse 0x048b4f79

                            2 (---          ) N 5 R 0x048b4f79

            2 32fe4b1b-2599-4283-addd-1e07de7232ae Inverse 0x048b4f79

                            5 (---          ) N 4 R 0x048b4f79

         *  3 00d10061-2498-4762-9783-5a2f41855d55 Inverse 0x048b4f79

                            4 (---          ) N 3 R 0x048b4f79

            4 5bb9e0f2-7e08-49e9-9abb-eaa3ab5c4495 Inverse 0x048b4f79

                            3 (---          ) N 2 R 0x048b4f79

            5 4127a97a-f4d7-4ad4-8f2b-ce379e430d89 Inverse 0x048b4f79

                            1 (---          ) Y 1 R 0x048b4f79

...

The value in the H column is always N (none) and it turns to Y when the consist
advertises the Head flag. The Discovery Lock column has 3 values: the first is the
position number, the second is the orientation respect to the composition at the time of
the Lock operation (S for direct, R for reversed, N if the Lock is not set), the last is the
CRC of the Train Composition Table at the time of the Lock operation.

The device can be configured to reboot automatically after the Composition Lock
operation ended successfully. When the following command is present in the
configuration, the device reboots after the specified number of minutes has passed
since the last successful Composition Lock operation.

      configure tcds lock reboot timeout <MINUTES>

If a Composition Unlock operation is performed in the mean time, the reboot timer is
cleared and no reboot happens.

21.7 Multiple coaches in TTCMP Projects

A TTCMP Train Project statically describes all the devices present in the network and
their interconnection and has the limitation that it can describe only a static network
topology in which the composition in terms of devices and interconnections is fixed.

This limit has been overcome by the introduction of the Multi-coach TTCMP projects.
These kind of projects describe the train topology in terms of sub-sets of the train
network, called Coaches. Each Coach, that is an indivisible unit, has no fixed position in
the in the actual train composition and can be present multiple times and with
different orientation.

Each Coach is treated by the TCDS in all respects as a Consist; thus all its devices must
share the same Consist id, that must be unique at train level.

184/253                                     Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                                                                     Coupling solutions

21.7.1 Coach Identifier and Description

The way the Consist Switch can recognize at deploy time to which kind of Coach
described in a Multi-coach TTCMP Project it belongs to is the Coach Identifier.

The Coach Identifier is an integer value ranging form 1 to 63 that marks a device as
belonging to a certain type of coach (i.e. locomotive, 1st class car, 2nd class car, dining
car, etc...); the value 0 means that no identifier has been set. An optional Coach
Description human readable string can be set together with the Identifier; the length of
this string is limited to 60 characters.
The Coach Identifier and its optional Description can be set by the commands

      sysadmin set coach-id <0-63> [ description "Coach Description" ]

and removed by

      sysadmin reset coach-id

If the Description contains white spaces, it must be enclosed in double quotes.

The values set in a device can be inspected with the show ttcmp command:

      A@C-SW-1>show ttcmp

      Global TTCMP information:
          TTCMP protocol version 2 extensions are enabled
          TTCMP subsystem status is: idle
          TTCMP packets signature is: disabled
          TTCMP matching mode is: default
          Consist identifier: undefined
          Coach identifier: 5 (Traction Unit)
          Composition Head flag: not set

      ...

      A@H-SW-2>show ttcmp

      Global TTCMP information:
          TTCMP protocol version 2 extensions are enabled
          TTCMP subsystem status is: idle
          TTCMP packets signature is: disabled
          TTCMP matching mode is: default
          Consist identifier: undefined
          Coach identifier: not set
          Composition Head flag: not set

      ...

The train wide status of the Coach Identifier can be queried by the command

      show tcds composition

that reports the status of all the consists, as shown in the following example:

          Discovery information
          -----------------------

              TCDS discovery is complete
              Active TCDS mode is plain
              TTCMP forwarding is enabled
              Number of discovered consists: 5
              CRC computed on local table: 0x048b4f79

186/253                     Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                       Managing device firmware

Chapter 22 - Managing device
  firmware

The system can hold a number of different firmware images. Images are stored in the
device flash memory and the number of images that a system can hold depends on
the flash size.
At any time on the system there are at least two images:

    the default image, which holds the firmware the system will load and boot at
      startup

    a hidden fallback image, used by the system in the boot up sequence, should the
      other image load fail

You can list the images installed on the system with the command sysadmin show
images, like in the following example:

      A@SW-Std.03-00-CA>sysadmin show images
      sw-std_1.5.0-4938.ksi [11.0M]
      sw-std_1.5.0-4943.ksi [11.0M] [Default image]

      Free image space: 37.5M

The command shows also the amount of free space left on the flash.
In order to load a firmware image on the system, you can use the command

      sysadmin load URL

where URL points to a Konuendo Archive Data (KAD) file on a reachable network
resource. Valid network schemes in the URL are ftp, http, tftp and scp. In case a network
resource should need authentication to be accessed, username and password
credentials must be specified in the URL. For example:

    A@SW-Std.03-00-CA>sysadmin load scp://user:password@192.168.1.1/path/to/image.kad

To change the default image, use the command

      sysadmin set default image NAME

where NAME is the name of one of the images listed by the sysadmin show images
command.
To remove an image from the system, use the command

      sysadmin delete image NAME

where NAME is the name of one of the images listed by the sysadmin show images
command.
The default image can be removed from the system provided that you set another
image as default before deleting it.

                                                              Device LED status and boot sequence

Chapter 25 - Device LED status and
  boot sequence

25.1 Service LEDs

On the front panel of the switch there are 8 service LEDs (POWER, FAULT and six
numbered LEDs) used to notify specific system states or events.

Each network port has two LEDs, one for the link state and one for PoE state (if
supported by the product).

POWER LED is steady green if the system has been correctly connected to a proper
power source and is operating.

FAULT LED notifies critical conditions in which device operation is compromised and
possible replacement is needed.

LEDs numbered from 1 to 6 are intended to provide a visual feedback of the current
logic state of the device. This will enable you to acknowledge at a glimpse if the switch
logic is working properly or not.

LED Function

1  System state

2  TTCMP© state

3  Reserved

4  Coupled-switch© state

5  Reserved

6  Miscellaneous info (boot fw status, transient ops, etc)

Table 8: System LEDs

25.2 Ports LEDs

Each network port has one or two bi-color LEDs:
    a green/yellow one (all ports) which indicates that the network link is up (steady)
      or that traffic is flowing through that link (blinking).

      Its actual color gives information about the link speed, with the green color used
      for the port nominal speed (100 Mbps for FE ports and 1 Gbps for GbE ports).

      For FE ports the LED will be yellow for a link speed of 10 Mbps. For GbE ports the

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4                       197/253
Device LED status and boot sequence

      LED will be yellow for a link speed of 100 Mbps, green-yellow for a speed of
      10Mbps.
    a green/red one used for PoE signaling (PoE equipped ports only).
      A steady green indicates that the port is supplying power to a PoE-capable device
      while a red PoE LED signals a problem in the power supply phase.
      This LED is marked with a special symbol on the panel.

25.3 LEDs and boot sequence

When you connect the device to the power source, the POWER LED becomes steady
green and the system starts the boot sequence:
1. internal logic is being warmed up and FAULT LED and LED #1 become steady red for

   one second
2. the system then performs a general hardware check switching on and off every

   second all the LEDs (except for POWER) in the 2 possible colors
3. when the system is ready to boot, all LEDs go off, except for FAULT LED and LED #1

   which become steady red again
4. while the system is loading the firmware image, the FAULT LED blinks red, while LED

   #1 changes from steady red to blinking red, then to blinking green
5. if bootstrap succeeded, FAULT LED is off and LED #1 is steady green
POWER LED state will remain unchanged during the above sequence.

25.4 LED n.6

A blinking red LED n.6 is a signal of some corrective action made during the boot
phase.
In detail, if the red LED is blinking slow, the first recovering action has been taken to
boot the device properly. The system is working correctly, but it is advisable to operate
a firmware update or re-install to fix the situation.
If the red LED is blinking fast, the second level of actions has been performed, meaning
that the device has gone through some serious degradation. The system is still
working properly, but some action (firmware update o re-install) to fix the situation has
to be taken immediately, because the system cannot stand a further corruption.

198/253  Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                                   Configuring the console

Chapter 26 - Configuring the console

This chapter describes how to connect a PC to the Consist Switch in order to perform
offline maintenance operations or out-of-band access to the command-line interface.

The required setup for connecting a terminal emulator to the device console varies
depending on the operating system in use on the PC . You can follow the instructions
given in the section below which applies to your platform.

The following settings apply to all platforms:

speed:                                 115200 bps
data bits:                             8
parity:                                none
stop-bits:                             1
terminal type:                         VT100

Before setting up and running the terminal emulator, you need to attach the
USB-to-console cable to the CONSOLE M12 connector on the Consist Switch.

IMPORTANT NOTE

Starting with S/N 151350, the interaction with the console driver for both
Linux and Windows OS has changed.

In the following chapters we will refer to the different approaches as the old
and the new method.

26.1 Terminal emulator setup on Linux

Old method

The software drivers needed to connect to the CONSOLE port of the Consist Switch are
normally found in recent Linux distributions. After plugging the cable you should be
able to find a new virtual communication port on the system.

You can easily find out the name of this new port using the dmesg command:

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4                                        199/253
Configuring the console

     # dmesg
     .......
     [ 165041.483643] usb 2-4: Number of endpoints 2
     [165041.483645] usb 2-4: Endpoint 1 MaxPacketSize 64
     [165041.483647] usb 2-4: Endpoint 2 MaxPacketSize 64
     [165041.483649] usb 2-4: Setting MaxPacketSize 64
     [165041.485738] usb 2-4: FTDI USB Serial Device converter now attached to ttyUSB0

In the above example the virtual communication port has been named /dev/ttyUSB0.

With this information it is possible to use one of the standard Linux terminal emulation
application (like Minicom or Kermit, for example) to access the user interface of the
Consist Switch.

New method

The software drivers needed to connect to the CONSOLE port of the Consist Switch are
normally found in recent Linux distributions. After plugging the cable you should be
able to find a new virtual communication port on the system.

You can easily find out the name of this new port using the dmesg command:

     # dmesg
     .......
      [44191.465444] usb 1-1.2: Number of endpoints 2
      [44191.465446] usb 1-1.2: Endpoint 1 MaxPacketSize 64
      [44191.465447] usb 1-1.2: Endpoint 2 MaxPacketSize 64
      [44191.465448] usb 1-1.2: Setting MaxPacketSize 64
      [44191.465947] usb 1-1.2: FTDI USB Serial Device converter now attached to ttyUSB0
      [44191.465966] ftdi_sio 1-1.2:1.1: FTDI USB Serial Device converter detected
      [44191.465992] usb 1-1.2: Detected FT2232C
      [44191.465994] usb 1-1.2: Number of endpoints 2
      [44191.465997] usb 1-1.2: Endpoint 1 MaxPacketSize 64
      [44191.465999] usb 1-1.2: Endpoint 2 MaxPacketSize 64
      [44191.466001] usb 1-1.2: Setting MaxPacketSize 64
      [44191.467073] usb 1-1.2: FTDI USB Serial Device converter now attached to ttyUSB1

In the above example two virtual communication ports has been created, named
/dev/ttyUSB0 and /dev/ttyUSB1.

The port to be used is the one with the greater number, so in this case is the
/dev/ttyUSB1.

With this information it is possible to use one of the standard Linux terminal emulation
application (like Minicom or Kermit, for example) to access the user interface of the
Consist Switch.

Old method

Use the RES_1.0 package in vdssw-ftdi-driver.zip archive to install the Console-to-USB
driver. The archive can be downloaded from the VDS Rail web site.

On Windows Host, check 'Ports' from the 'Device Manager'. It should display some new
USB serial ports with a COM port number. The highest COM port number is used for

                                       Configuring the console

console. Steps to verify this:.
    Right click on My Computer.
    Select Properties option.
    Select Hardware tab.

 Expand Ports (COM & LPT) label as shown in the figure below. As you can see in

                                                                              Configuring the console

New method

On a Windows based PC a driver must be installed. This can be done in 2 different
ways:

    · If the PC is connected to Internet, the first time a console is connected, a
         guided procedure automatically downloads and installs the needed software.

    · Otherwise the driver installation can be done starting from the driver package.
         This package can be downloaded from the manufacturer's site
         (http://www.ftdichip.com/Drivers/VCP.htm) selecting the VCP driver as "setup
         executable".
         This setup must be launched and the driver installation process takes place.
         Once finished you can connect to the Consist Switch Console.
         In both cases, two serial device will be created, usually called COM3 and COM4.
         Only one of the two is working, generally the last one, and through this COM
         port it will be possible to interact with the switch console.

On the PC a terminal emulator must be used. A recommended application is Putty
(http://www.putty.org/).

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4           203/253
                                                                                     Password Recovery

Chapter 27 - Password Recovery

This chapter describes how to restore the Consist Switch original factory passwords in
case you forget yours.

     NOTE
     All the other saved configurations are left untouched.
To execute this procedure follow these steps:
    While connected to the console, power cycle the system.
    When the system reboots, wait until the following message appears on the

          console:

                Enter password - autoboot in 2 seconds...

    Insert the password vds
    At the boot-loader prompt (vds:), execute the command:

                vds: run rescue

    The system will boot and then automatically reboot.
    At the end of the operation the system will have all the factory set passwords

          restored.

     NOTE
     During the operation some harmless messages may be output on the screen
     (eg: "umount: can't umount ...").

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  205/253
                                                              Konuendo Total Device Management

Chapter 28 - Konuendo Total Device
  Management

The Konuendo Total Device Management (KTDM) is a technology that gives you the
possibility to handle the maintenance operations starting from a switched-off device.
The operations available are, among the others, software installations, software
upgrade/downgrade, configuration (or other data) injection, custom operations, etc.
KTDM needs to be run from a USB pen-drive prepared as described below and may
be used in a menu-based fashion or in a total unattended way.
KTDM is distributed in the form of a ZIP archive whose content must be extracted into
an empty pen-drive base directory.
The USB pen-drive (preferably USB 2.0 compliant) must have at least 128 MB of free
space and, in case of multiple partitions, only the first one will be used.
The files contained in the archive are, at least:

    bigbang-a.ksi, the KTDM main driver.
    ipart.kad, the system image to be loaded on the device.
    u-boot.kad, the bootloader image.
Other files may optionally be present according to the operation needs.

28.1 Firmware upgrade procedure

    Prepare the USB pen-drive as detailed before.
    Turn off the Consist Switch
    Plug the previously prepared pen-drive into the system maintenance port

          (labeled MNT- H or MGMT)
    Turn on the device
    After the power-on phase, the green LEDs labeled 1 to 6 should be switching

          on and off in a circular fashion.
    When the operation is complete, all the LEDs will be steady green.
    At this point unplug the USB pen-drive and the Consist Switch will

          automatically reboot.
    In case of trouble, all the LEDs will be turned to steady red. In this case, rerun

          the operation and, if the problem persists, contact VDS Rail representatives for
          help.

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  207/253
Konuendo Total Device Management

                                WARNING
                                A POWER LOSS DURING THE FIRMWARE WRITING
                                STEP CAN BE VERY DANGEROUS AND MAY
                                COMPROMISE THE FIRMWARE IMAGE.

208/253  Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4
                                                              Konuendo Total Device Management

28.1.1 Firmware upgrade without losing
     saved device configuration

The operation described in the previous paragraph clears all the data on the Consist
Switch.
The following instructions let you perform the firmware upgrade operation without
losing the Consist Switch saved configuration:

    Prepare the USB pen-drive as detailed before.
    Plug the pen-drive into the system maintenance port (labeled MNT- H or

          MGMT).
    From the admin account, execute the command

                 A@SW-Std.03-00-CA>sysadmin save configfile usb:///

          and then

                 A@SW-Std.03-00-CA>sysadmin reboot

    Say "Y" to the "Press Y to confirm, N otherwise:" question.
    The Consist Switch will reboot.
    After the power-on phase, the green LEDs labeled 1 to 6 should be switching

          on and off in a circular fashion.
    When the operation is complete, all the LEDs will be steady green.
    At this point unplug the USB pen-drive and the Consist Switch will

          automatically reboot.
In case of trouble, all the LEDs will be turned to steady red. In this case, rerun the
operation and, if the problem persists, contact VDS Rail representatives for help.

     WARNING

     This operation saves the Consist Switch configuration on the pen-drive, as a
     by-product, in a file called CFG-20xxyyzztt.kad.

     If the pen-drive has to be reused for another upgrade operation, be sure to
     remove this CFG-20xxyyzztt.kad file from the pen-drive.

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  209/253
                                                                                    AutoUSB subsystem

Chapter 29 - AutoUSB subsystem

29.1 Description

AutoUSB is a mechanism to interact with the Consist Switch at run time to conduct
various activities like firmware upgrade, Consist-Project injection and network
configuration, simple reset or reboot actions, etc.
Those actions are pre-defined and are activated by the simple connection of a special
prepared USB pen-drive to the MNT-H or MGMT connector.
This mechanism is extremely powerful, so, if needed, it can be disabled with a simple
configuration command.
Later a list of all the defined actions is presented.

29.2 USB pen-drive setup

    1. The USB pen-drive must have at least one VFAT partition bigger than 128 MB.
         The name of this first partition must be: VDS_KACTION
         On a Windows machine the partition Label may be easily changed from a File
         Explorer with a Rename operation.
         On a Linux computer one way to rename the partition label is using the tool
         called gparted.

    2. The possible actions are distributed, on request, via a Zip file.
    3. Once this zip file is extracted in the main folder of the first USB pen-drive

         partition, a folder called apps is created.
         This folder contains a few files, some called K_ACTION... are the actual
         procedures while others, INFO_....txt files, are help files.
    4. Depending on the function required, the relevant K_ACTION... file has to be
         copied to the main folder. So, for example, if we want to just reboot a device,
         the main USB pen-drive folder will contain the subfolder apps and the
         function K_ACTION.x.y.simple_reboot.
    5. Some other file might be needed depending on the required function. See the
         list below for specific instructions.

29.3 Operation

Once the USB pen-drive is set-up, the activation is very easy.

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  211/253
AutoUSB subsystem

    1. Plug the USB pen-drive in the connector labeled as MNT-H or MGMT by means
         of the M12-USB adaptation cable.

    2. When the function starts, the LED n. 6 starts to blink green.
    3. When the LED n.6 turns steady green, the function completed its actions and

         the USB pen-drive may be disconnected.

     WARNING:

     While this mechanism is very easy and powerful, it show little or no feed-back
     on the on-going operations, except for the LED n.6 code.

If a better understanding of the actions progression is requested, a richer approach is
achieved using a diagnostic/configuration station loaded with the KonfStation tool by
VDS Rail (ask VDS Rail representatives for details).

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4                                                   213/253
                                                                                 External configuration

Chapter 30 - External configuration

The External configuration mode allows to rely on configuration setup stored in an
external USB pen-drive, so that no detail is maintained in the switch internal storage.
If the External configuration mode has been chosen, an USB pen-drive is needed in
order to save the configuration commands externally.
The USB pen-drive (preferably USB 2.0 compliant) must have at least 128 MB of free
space and, in case of multiple partitions, only the first one will be used.
Follow these steps for the External configuration mode:

    1. Configure the Consist Switch the way you need.
    2. Save the configuration with the command

                A@SW-Std.03-00-CA>save running-config

    3. Do not switch off the device.
    4. Plug the pen-drive into the system maintenance port (labeled MNT- H or

         MGMT).
    5. From the CLI issue the command

                A@SW-Std.03-00-CA>sysadmin save configfile usb:/// external

The configuration now has been saved on the external pen-drive so, as long as the
pen-drive is plugged in, the switch will boot with the externally saved configuration
details, no matter how the switch will be reconfigured afterward.
If a new configuration setup is needed, the procedure must be re-done starting from
point 1.
If the USB pen-drive is unplugged, the Consist Switch will boot with the internally
saved configuration, if any.
If the USB pen-drive is plugged into another Consist Switch, the same considerations
apply for the new device.

 WARNING

 During a boot with a pen-drive plugged in, a message is printed on the console.
 This message:

 ** Unable to read "bigbang-a.ksi" from usb 0:1 **

 is totally harmless.

Consist Switch User Manual ver. 2.0.4  215/253

     Enable auto-logout. Default is 10 seconds.

     Clear the packet counters for the LLDP protocol.

     Clear the packet counters for the TTCMP protocol.

     Clear all active DHCP server/snooping bindings.

     Clear the active DHCP server/snooping bindings for the given interface.

  clear mac-address

     Clear all MAC addresses detected from network and stored in memory.

     Clear all MAC addresses detected from the given network interface.

  clear topology

     Clear the list of nodes collected by means of the TTCMP protocol.

configure ip filter NAME

   Manage the definition of the filter called NAME.

configure ip filter NAME RULE# accept|deny icmp-ping|ip SRC-ADDRESS|any
                      INGRESS-INTERFACE|any DEST-ADDRESS|any EGRESS-INTERFACE|

any

   Define a rule, identified with the number RULE#, for the given filter. It can be
   a rule accepting or denying the matching packets, matching IP or ICMP
   routed traffic. The matching traffic is defined by the source address (SRC-
   ADDRESS), ingress interface (INGRES-INTERFACE), destination address (DEST-
   ADDRESS) and egress interface (EGRESS-INTERFACE).

configure ip filter NAME RULE# accept|deny icmp-ping|ip SRC-ADDRESS|any
                                                                                INGRESS-INTERFACE|any

local

   Define a rule, identified with the number RULE#, for the given filter. It can be
   a rule accepting or denying the matching packets, matching IP or ICMP
   locally destined traffic. The matching traffic is defined by the source address
   (SRC-ADDRESS) and ingress interface (INGRES-INTERFACE).

configure ip filter NAME RULE# accept|deny tcp|udp
                                         SRC-ADDRESS|any SRC-PORT|any INGRESS-INTERFACE|any

                                        DEST-ADDRESS|any DEST-PORT|any EGRESS-INTERFACE|any

   Define a rule, identified with the number RULE#, for the given filter. It can be
   a rule accepting or denying the matching packets, matching TCP or UDP
   routed traffic. The matching traffic is defined by the source address (SRC-
   ADDRESS), source port (SRC-PORT), ingress interface (INGRES-INTERFACE),
   destination address (DEST-ADDRESS), destination port (DEST-PORT), and
   egress interface (EGRESS-INTERFACE).

   and 4 the one with highest priority.

                                                               broadcast VALUE K|M|G burst auto|

BURST

   Define a rate limit for broadcast packets with explicit committed burst size
   BURST expressed in Bytes. The keyword auto will trigger the automatic
   computation of an optimal burst size (but carrying out this operation
   requires up to some seconds).

   Define a rate limit for inbound packets. By default the rate VALUE is
   expressed in Mbps; optionally the value can be expressed in kbps.

   Configure Routing Information Protocol (RIP): basic update timers. INTERVAL
   is the time between updates with the given TIMEOUT and and FLUSH time.
   All are in seconds and in the range 5-2147483.

   Configure Routing Information Protocol (RIP): set the protocol version in use.

     ones available in the global family. Set ALL as TYPES to activate all the traps

     values: overtemp, high-cpu-load, configuration-change, sysadmin-change,
     new-root and topology-change.

     ones available in the ttcmp family. Specify ALL as TYPES to activate all the
     traps available for this family or use a comma-separated list of the following
     values: ttcmp-anomaly.
